How to Use This Inventory

  • Owner separates core platform surfaces from bundled system extensions.
  • User feature names what a user or external agent can actually do.
  • Current test hooks lists obvious automated tests, smoke scripts, or docs found during the audit.
  • Coverage is TBD until a later pass assigns a percentage from concrete assertions or coverage data.
  • QA notes call out app-path, visual, live integration, or manual checks that should not be inferred from unit tests.
  • Live app-path QA must inspect the whole composed viewport, including inactive split panes and restored workbench tabs.

    • A raw stack trace in any visible panel is a finding even when the primary route/control works.

  • QA status ledger lives in docs/feature-qa-status.md. Use it to track each feature as

    • Inventory only, Automated only, Live QA partial, or Live QA pass.

  • A feature should not be considered Live QA pass unless the row records the actual user-visible path exercised, the

    • latest evidence date or screenshot/log artifact, the relevant focused tests/builds, and no known remaining gaps.

Primary validation command families found during the audit:

Scope Command

Chat E2E smoke pnpm run e2e:chat / pnpm run test:e2e:runner --grep @chat
Desktop fork/rewind smoke pnpm run smoke:desktop:fork-rewind / pnpm run test:e2e:runner --grep @fork-rewind
Desktop performance smoke pnpm run perf:desktop
Extension webapp smoke pnpm run smoke:extension-webapps
Model gateway smoke pnpm run smoke:model-gateway

Registered Extension Directory Exception

extensions/system-neon-pilot-agent exists in this checkout only as a dist/ directory without a source extension.json, README, or tests. It was not treated as a registered system extension in this inventory. Revisit if packaging discovers it another way.

Optional or External First-Party Surfaces Mentioned by Docs

The repository docs mention optional first-party extensions distributed from patleeman/neon-pilot-extensions; their source is not present in this checkout. They should be inventoried from that repository or installed package artifacts before assigning coverage percentages.

2026-06-26 Parent QA Notes

  • Queued follow-up and direct-bash hydration: parent follow-up found that the direct-bash route treated a running whole-line bash command as a hard composer lock, so the user could not reliably queue the next normal prompt while the command was running. The UI now keeps the composer available during whole-line bash, stores a normal submitted prompt as a post-bash follow-up, and drains it through the conversation message path once the matching bash block completes. Attempting a second whole-line bash command while one is already running now shows user-facing guidance instead of silently doing nothing. Parent then launched the rebuilt Testing app directly on isolated CDP 9667, skipped first-run onboarding through the visible UI, ran a long !!sh -c command, typed and submitted a normal follow-up while bash was running, and verified the follow-up drained after bash completion.
